            "quote": "The Alicyclobacillus sp. as sulfur-oxidizing bacteria (SOB) and the Sulfobacillus sp. as iron-oxidizing bacteria (IOB) were utilized for bioleaching the spent LIBs in the form of mixed culture after acclimation of 30 d by contact with the spent LIB powder at dose ranging from 0.25% to 1.0% (w/v) (higher dose of over 1.0% evidently inhibited the growth of both cells). The detailed procedures and methods about screen, culture, maintain, inoculum and identification of the SOB and IOB were described in the previous paper (Xin et al., 2011)."
          },
          {
            "file": "source/text.md",
            "line": 43,
            "quote": "Bioleaching experiments were conducted according to the previous paper (Xin et al., 2009). Unless specifically stated, bioleaching media containing 4.0 g L ^-1 of mixed energy matters (sulfur and pyrite) was inoculated respectively with SOB and IOB as the mixed culture, followed by incubation at 30 \u0003C until the pH value dropped to ca 1.0 and the ORP value rose to ca 450 mV. At that time, the spent LIB powder was added into the media to a final concentration of 2% to initiate the bioleaching after its pH value was adjusted precisely to pH 1.0. Three pulp densities (1%, 2% and 4%) were set to examine bioleaching behavior of Co and Li when pulp density increased from 1% to 4%. Three doses of mixed energy substrates (2.0, 4.0 and 8.0 g L ^-1) were set to assess the feasibility of improving extraction of Co and Li by lifting energy matters dose. The pH value of the media was controlled at pH 2.0 during bioleaching by adding H2S04 to assess the possibility of suitable pH for cells growth to promote extraction of Co and Li (bioleaching dose was obtained by measured dose subtracted released one in blank flasks with addition of the same amount of exogenous H2S04). Three bioleaching temperatures (30, 35, and 40 ^∘C) were set to inspect the feasibility of rising temperature to improve performance. The suitable controls including cells-free media, pure H2S04 solution at pH 1.0, H2SO4 at t pH 1.0 + FeSO4 at 2.0 g L ^-1, H2S04 at pH 1.0 + Fe2 (SO4)3 at 2.0 g L ^-1 were run. During bioleaching, release dose of Co and Li, Fe ^2+ and Fe ^3+ dosage, pH, ORP and cell number were periodically monitored. All experiments were carried out in triplicates."
          }

          "chunk_id": "CHUNK_Process Controls for Improving Bioleaching Performance of Both Li and Co from Spent Lithium Ion Batteries at High Pulp Density and its Thermodynamics and Kineti_2.3. Bioleaching experiments_f9e3f839",
          "text": "## 2.3. Bioleaching experiments\nBioleaching experiments were conducted according to the previous paper (Xin et al., 2009). Unless specifically stated, bioleaching media containing 4.0 g L ^-1 of mixed energy matters (sulfur and pyrite) was inoculated respectively with SOB and IOB as the mixed culture, followed by incubation at 30 \u0003C until the pH value dropped to ca 1.0 and the ORP value rose to ca 450 mV. At that time, the spent LIB powder was added into the media to a final concentration of 2% to initiate the bioleaching after its pH value was adjusted precisely to pH 1.0. Three pulp densities (1%, 2% and 4%) were set to examine bioleaching behavior of Co and Li when pulp density increased from 1% to 4%. Three doses of mixed energy substrates (2.0, 4.0 and 8.0 g L ^-1) were set to assess the feasibility of improving extraction of Co and Li by lifting energy matters dose. The pH value of the media was controlled at pH 2.0 during bioleaching by adding H2S04 to assess the possibility of suitable pH for cells growth to promote extraction of Co and Li (bioleaching dose was obtained by measured dose subtracted released one in blank flasks with addition of the same amount of exogenous H2S04). Three bioleaching temperatures (30, 35, and 40 ^∘C) were set to inspect the feasibility of rising temperature to improve performance. The suitable controls including cells-free media, pure H2S04 solution at pH 1.0, H2SO4 at t pH 1.0 + FeSO4 at 2.0 g L ^-1, H2S04 at pH 1.0 + Fe2 (SO4)3 at 2.0 g L ^-1 were run. During bioleaching, release dose of Co and Li, Fe ^2+ and Fe ^3+ dosage, pH, ORP and cell number were periodically monitored. All experiments were carried out in triplicates.",

          "chunk_id": "CHUNK_Process Controls for Improving Bioleaching Performance of Both Li and Co from Spent Lithium Ion Batteries at High Pulp Density and its Thermodynamics and Kineti_3. Result and discussion_dd88c392",
          "text": "## 3. Result and discussion\n3.1. Leaching behavior of Co and Li from spent LIBs under varied pulp densities\nPulp density displayed notable influence on bioleaching performance of both Co and Li from spent LIBs. When pulp densities increased from 1% to 4%, the bioleaching efficiency decreased from 52% to 10% for Co and from 80% to 37% for Li after a culture period of 11 d, respectively (Fig. 1). As for released dose, however, Li elevated from 360 to 660 mg L^-1;; whereas Co fell from 1862 to 1432 mg L ^-1 (Fi g. 1). On the other hand, pulp density also greatly affected variation behavior of both pH and ORP of the bioleaching media. In general, the pH value rose and ORP value dropped due to massive consumption of H ^+ for mobilization of both Co and Li (Fi g. 2). Further, it was found that higher pulp density resulted in higher pH value and lower ORP value. With increase of the pulp density from 1% to 4%, pH value increased from 1.97 to 4.46 and ORP value decreased from 509 to 335 mV after incubation of 11 d, respectively (Fig. 2). By contrast, the sterile control kept no dissolution of Li and Co throughout bioleaching; of the three chemical leaching controls, H2SO4 + FeSO4 harvested the maximum Co yield (9%) and H2S04 + Fe2 (S04)3 obtained the maximum Li efficiency (17%) at 2% of pulp density.\nIn the past studies, Acidithiobacillus ferrooxidans only as IOB was used for leaching spent LIBs (Mishra et al., 2008a; Zeng et al., 2012). In the case of 1% sulfur + 3 gL ^-1 ferrous as the mixed energy substrates, 56% of Co dissolution and 8% of Li release were attained at pulp density of 1% after 20 d culture, in which a possible reduction of insoluble Co^3+ into soluble Co^2+ by unknown intermediate reducing compounds which might come from sulfur metabolism was put forward as bioleaching mechanism (Mishra et al., 2008a). In the case of ferrous at 9 g L ^-1 as sole energy substrates, 43% of Co extraction was achieved after 10 d of culture at 1% of pulp density (Zeng et al., 2012). However, the Co release rose up to 99% in the presence of copper catalysis and a completely different mechanism regarding Co extraction was proposed that formulate as following (Zeng et al., 2012):\nHigh pulp density could significantly reduce the amount of bioleaching media and the bioleaching cost. The bioleaching performance of the mixed culture was assessed at high pulp densities of more than 1%, and an evident decline in bioleaching efficiency was observed for both Co and Li with increase of pulp density from 1% to 4% (Fig. 1). The spent LIBs contained not only LiCoO2 as electrode but also toxic and flammable electrolyte, an organic liquid with dissolved substances like LiClO4, LiBF4 and LiPF6 (Xu et al., 2008). At higher pulp density, much more amount of toxic electrolyte was dissolved into bioleaching media, endangering the growth and activity of both kinds of bacteria in spite of spent LIBs-tolerance (Fig. 3). Lower activity of SOB resulted in less generation of H2S04 and thereby higher pH value of media; lower activities of IOB led to less amount of Fe ^ 3̅ + and thereby lower ORP value of media (Fig. 2). Li release mainly depended on the SOB because of acid dissolution as bioleaching mechanism; whereas Co liberation relied on both the SOB and IOB due to the combined action of both acid dissolution and Fe ^2+ mediated reduction as bioleaching mechanism (Xin et al., 2009). Therefore, great decline in bioleaching efficiency was occurred for both Co and Li when increase in pulp density (Fig. 1). In the following studies, the process controls were attempted to improve the bioleaching efficiency of Co and Li at pulp density of 2%.",

          "text": "## 3.4. Improving bioleaching performance ofspent LIBs at pulp density of 2% by increasing bioleaching temperature\nTemperature had a strong influence on the bioleaching performance of spent LIBs. When temperature increase from 30 to 35 ^∘𝖢,, released efficiency increased from 78% to 89% for Li and from 52% to 72% for Γ0, respectively (Fig. SM-1). However, higher temperature of 40 ^∘C adversely affected the activity and growth of the leaching cells because of the mesophilic nature, leading to the lowest ORP value and Fe ^3+ / Fe ^2+ (Fig. SM-2) as well as cell density (Fig. SM-3) which accounted for the lowest mobilization doses of Co and Li (Fig. SM-1). The results demonstrated that 35 \u0003C of temperature was optimum for the bioleaching of spent LIBs. By increasing the bioleaching temperature, adding the dose of mixed energy substrates and acid adjustment of media at pH 2.0, the bioleaching efficiency of both Co and Li was notably improved and the maximum extraction efficiency of 89% for Li and 72% for Co was obtained at pulp density of 2%, respectively, indicating the great potential of process controls to promote the bioleaching performance of spent LIBs. Nevertheless, higher extraction efficiency of more than 90% for both Co and Li at higher pulp density of over 2% (even 10%) was still a challenge, which was expected for the practical application. Changing the structure of LiCoO2 by pretreatments such as ultrasound, microwave and heat as well as acclimating high tolerant-electrolytes leaching strain was alternatives to further improve bioleaching performance of spent LIBs in the future studied.",

          "text": "## 3.7. Bioleaching mechanisms of Co and Li at pulp density of 2%\nThe bioleaching residual of the spent LIBs was analyzed using SEM and XRD and compared with the raw ones in order to reveal the bioleaching microcosmic mechanism. It was clear that the surface of spent LIBs particles grew rough and the boundary grew obscure (Fig. SM-4), indicating the occurrence of bioleaching. However, the particles did not disappear after bioleaching, accounting for remaining of Li and Co. The XRD analysis displayed that Li and Co existed in the form of LiCoO2 crystal in the raw spent LIBs, and the remaining Li and Co existed still in the form of LiCoO crystal after bioleaching (Fig. SM-5).\nDespite different chemical mechanisms accounting for bioleaching of spent LIBs, there were no reports on the dynamics, thermodynamics and micro-process of the bioleaching so far. The present works demonstrated that bioleaching of spent LIBs using elemental sulfur and pyrite as mixed energy substrates has much greater potential to occur spontaneously than traditional chemical leaching in the presence of H2S04 + FeSO4 / H2O2 for the first time based on thermodynamics analysis, and the product layer diffusion model described best the bioleaching behavior of both Co and Li from the spent LIBs.
